I don't feel tired at all and honestly don't feel the need to sleep any more than that.\n\nDoctor: During this time, has your high energy caused serious problems like being hospitalized, getting into major trouble, or making risky decisions that hurt your work, finances, or relationships?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that, to be honest. I haven't really thought about whether it has caused any major problems like that in my life. I've just been so focused on keeping busy and getting things done.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you been more talkative than usual, had racing thoughts, or felt unusually driven to keep doing activities?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely! I've been talking constantly and so fast that people can't seem to interrupt me, and I feel this unstoppable drive to keep starting new projects and activities. I'm not quite sure how to describe my thoughts, but my mind and body definitely feel like they are constantly on the go.\n\nDoctor: During this high-energy period, have you felt unusually powerful, important, or capable of things that other people would see as unrealistic?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've felt unusually powerful or capable of unrealistic things. It's more that I just have an overwhelming amount of energy and feel this constant, intense need to keep moving and doing things.\n\nDoctor: Has this high-energy period made you unable to function normally at work, school, or home, or has anyone urged you to get emergency help or be hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how much it has affected my normal functioning, since I've just been so focused on staying busy and getting things done. Nobody has suggested that I need emergency help or that I should be hospitalized, though.\n\nDoctor: At any point during these five months, did the high-energy symptoms last at least 4 days but not clearly cause major problems, or was there ever a stretch of at least 1 week where they clearly disrupted your life?"

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ manic_episode\n  - Elevated Mood: A mood that is abnormally high, euphoric, and full of unmotivated happiness and vitality.\n  - Irritable Mood: A state where one gets angry or aggressive even at minor stimuli.\n  - Mood Lability: Rapid shifts in mood, alternating unpredictably between euphoria and irritability.\n  - Increased Energy: Overflowing physical and mental energy, leading to an inability to stay still and a desire to keep moving.\n  - Hyperactivity: Doing far more work than usual or starting multiple activities simultaneously.\n  - Psychomotor Agitation: Inability to tolerate resting or stopping, driven by a constant need to do something.\n  - Reduced Sleep Duration: Sleeping significantly less than usual but feeling refreshed rather than tired.\n  - Loss of Sleep Need: Not feeling the urge to sleep or the thought that sleep is necessary.\n  - Indifference to Insomnia: Feeling no problem and continuing activities despite staying up for several nights.\n  -
